- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 9

- **Script:** This code looks perfect. It won't compile. GCC says: stray backslash 3 4 2 in program. Why? Look at the quotes. They're curly quotes, copied from a website. ## EP 02 - What is C, really?

### EP 02 S1 - Your phone runs on C
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–2
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Your phone is running 50-year-old code."
- **Script:** Your phone is running 50-year-old code. So is your laptop. So is the small computer in a car engine. The language is C, created in 1972, and it's still everywhere. Want to know why? Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 02 S2 - What a compiler does
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Computers don't understand C. Here's the trick."
- **Script:** Computers don't understand C. Here's the trick. A CPU only understands machine code: ones and zeroes. So you write C in a text file, called source code. A program called a compiler translates the whole file into machine code, an exe file the CPU runs directly. Translate once, run forever. That's why C is fast. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** C → COMPILER → 1s AND 0s
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 02
- **Instagram caption:** What a compiler actually does, in 40 seconds. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #compiler #learntocode #kodingkonkani #computerscience
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** What a compiler does | C Programming EP 02 - What a compiler actually does, in 40 seconds. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #compiler #learntocode #kodingkonkani #computerscience #shorts

- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 8

## EP 03 - Setting up: VS Code + GCC on Windows

### EP 03 S1 - 'gcc' is not recognized?
- **Length / source:** 0:45 · built from scenes 1, 6, 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Windows doesn't know gcc? Fix it in 40 seconds."
- **Script:** Windows doesn't know gcc? Fix it in 40 seconds. Search for "Edit the system environment variables". Click Environment Variables. Select Path, click Edit, then New. Type C colon, msys64, ucrt64, bin. OK, OK, OK. Now close your terminal and open a new one. Type gcc dash dash version. There's your version number. Fixed. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 'gcc' NOT RECOGNIZED? FIX IT
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 03
- **Instagram caption:** The fix for "'gcc' is not recognized" on Windows. Save this. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #gcc #windows #kodingkonkani #codingtips #learntocode
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** 'gcc' is not recognized? | C Programming EP 03 - The fix for "'gcc' is not recognized" on Windows. Save this. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #gcc #windows #kodingkonkani #codingtips #learntocode #shorts

### EP 03 S2 - What is PATH?
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Your computer has a secret list of folders."
- **Script:** Your computer has a secret list of folders. It's called PATH. When you type a command like gcc, Windows searches every folder on that list, in order. If the program's folder isn't there, Windows says it has never heard of it. Add the folder, reopen the terminal, and it works. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** PATH = WHERE WINDOWS LOOKS
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 03
- **Instagram caption:** PATH explained in 30 seconds. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #windows #learntocode #kodingkonkani #computerscience
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** What is PATH? | C Programming EP 03 - PATH explained in 30 seconds. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #windows #learntocode #kodingkonkani #computerscience #shorts

### EP 03 S3 - Reopen the terminal!
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scene 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "You set PATH and it still fails. Here's why."
- **Script:** You set PATH and gcc still isn't recognized. Here's why. A terminal reads PATH only when it opens. Your old window never saw the change. Close it, close VS Code, open them again. And check your entry ends in backslash bin. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

### EP 03 S5 - Which folder goes in PATH?
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 6, 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Only one of these PATH entries works. Which one?"
- **Script:** Only one of these PATH entries works. A: C colon msys64 ucrt64. B: C colon msys64 ucrt64 bin. C: C colon msys46 ucrt64 bin. Comment your letter. Answer: B. gcc dot exe lives inside the bin folder, and option C has a typo. **Comment your answer, then follow for the solution.**

## EP 04 - Hello, World!

### EP 04 S1 - Your first C program
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1, 4, 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Six lines. Your first program. Let's go."
- **Script:** Six lines. Your first program. Let's go. Make a file called hello dot c. Type hash include stdio dot h, int main, printf Hello World with backslash n, and return zero. In the terminal: gcc hello dot c dash o hello. Then dot slash hello. Hello, World! Welcome to programming. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** YOUR FIRST C PROGRAM

### EP 04 S2 - Hello World, explained
- **Length / source:** 0:45 · built from scene 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Every line of Hello World, in 40 seconds."
- **Script:** Every line of Hello World, in 40 seconds. Hash include stdio dot h brings in printf. int main is where the program starts, and int means it returns a whole number. Curly braces hold main's code. printf prints the text in quotes, and backslash n moves to a new line. The semicolon ends the statement. return zero means everything went fine. Done. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 04 S3 - The error is on the line above
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "The error mentions return. The bug isn't there."
- **Script:** The error mentions return. The bug isn't there. GCC says: expected semicolon before return. Return is on line five, but the missing semicolon is at the end of line four. The compiler only notices it when it hits the next word. So always check the line above. Even the caret points to the end of line four. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** CHECK THE LINE ABOVE
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 04

## EP 05 - How a C Program Is Built

### EP 05 S1 - What happens when you press Enter?
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1, 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "You press Enter. Four things happen."
- **Script:** You press Enter. Four things happen. One: the preprocessor pastes in your include files and deletes comments. Two: the compiler turns C into assembly. Three: the assembler turns that into machine code. Four: the linker joins in library code like printf. Then you have a program. Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 1 PREPROCESS · 2 COMPILE · 3 ASSEMBLE · 4 LINK
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 05
- **Instagram caption:** What GCC really does in half a second. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #gcc #compiler
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** What happens when you press Enter? | C Programming EP 05 - What GCC really does in half a second. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #gcc #compiler #shorts

### EP 05 S2 - Four stages in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Dash E. Dash S. Dash c. Remember these."
- **Script:** Want to see each stage yourself? gcc dash capital E stops after the preprocessor: you get a dot i file with hundreds of lines. gcc dash capital S stops after compiling: you get assembly in a dot s file. gcc dash small c stops after assembling: you get an object file. Plain gcc goes all the way and links. Four stops, one bus. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** -E .i · -S .s · -c .o · gcc .exe
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 05
- **Instagram caption:** Get off the GCC bus at any stop. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #gcc #ctutorial
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Four stages in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 05 - Get off the GCC bus at any stop. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #gcc #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 05 S3 - My edit didn't work?!
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "I changed the code. Why is the output the same?"
- **Script:** You change the text. You save. You run the program. Same old output. Your code didn't change? It did. But you ran the old exe. The exe only updates when you compile again. Save, compile, run. Every time. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

### EP 05 S5 - Compiler or linker?
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 6, 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Who throws this error: compiler or linker?"
- **Script:** Who throws this error? Undefined reference to print. Compiler or linker? Think. ... It's the linker. "Undefined reference" means the linker searched the libraries and found nothing called print. Comment if you got it. **Comment your answer, then follow for the solution.**

### EP 06 S2 - Comments in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Two ways to write a comment in C."
- **Script:** Two ways to write a comment in C. Slash slash: everything after it on that line is ignored. Great for short notes. Slash star, then star slash: everything between them is ignored, even many lines. Great for a header at the top of your file. Comments explain why, not what. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 06 S3 - The comment that ate my code
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "This comment just ate my whole program."
- **Script:** This comment just ate my whole program. I opened it with slash star and forgot star slash. So printf, return, even the closing brace became part of the comment. GCC says: unterminated comment. Fix: close it, or use slash slash for one-line notes. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 08 - Data Types: int, float, char

### EP 08 S1 - Where did half a kilo go?
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "2.5 kg of fish. My program says 2."
- **Script:** Two and a half kilos of fish. My program says two. Where did the half go? I stored it in an int. An int only holds whole numbers, so C chops off the decimal. No rounding, no warning. Use a float, and the fish is back. **Follow for the next one.**

## EP 09 - More Types: long, double, unsigned

### EP 09 S1 - The odometer rolled back
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 6

- **Script:** Ninety-nine thousand nine hundred ninety-nine, plus one, equals zero? On an odometer, yes. It ran out of digits. C numbers do the same. An unsigned short holds up to sixty-five thousand five hundred thirty-five. Add one, and it wraps to zero. **Follow for the next one.**

- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3, 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "int not big enough? Here's what to use."
- **Script:** int not big enough? Use long long, 8 bytes, printed with percent l l d. Never need negatives? Use unsigned, printed with percent u. Need precise decimals? Use double instead of float. Pick the box that fits your number. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 09 S3 - 1 lakh x 1 lakh = wrong
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "One lakh times one lakh. C says 1.4 billion."
- **Script:** One lakh times one lakh should be ten billion. C says one point four billion. Why? Both numbers are int, so the answer must fit in an int. It doesn't, so it overflows. Fix: one hundred thousand LL times one hundred thousand, stored in a long long. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 10 - Constants & #define

### EP 10 S1 - The fare went up
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "The bus fare changed. Now fix six lines."
- **Script:** The bus fare went from fifteen to twenty. It's in six places in your code. You fix five. One is still wrong. Instead, write hash define BUS\_FARE fifteen at the top. Use the name everywhere. Next time, change one line. Done. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 10 S2 - const vs #define
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Two ways to make a constant in C."
- **Script:** Two ways to make a constant in C. const float PI equals three point one four: a real variable, with a type, that nobody can change. Hash define BUS\_FARE fifteen: the preprocessor swaps the name for the text before compiling. No type, no semicolon. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** const = LOCKED BOX · #define = FIND & REPLACE

### EP 10 S3 - The semicolon that breaks macros
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "One semicolon. Three errors."
- **Script:** One semicolon, three lines of errors. Hash define BUS\_FARE fifteen semicolon. The preprocessor copies the semicolon into printf, and GCC says expected closing bracket before semicolon. Look for "in expansion of macro". Fix: no semicolon after a hash define. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** WRONG: #define X 15; · RIGHT: #define X 15

## EP 11 - printf Deep Dive

### EP 11 S1 - Messy bill vs rich bill
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Same bill. One looks broke. One looks rich."
- **Script:** Same bill. One looks broke, one looks rich. The secret: widths inside printf. Percent minus twelve s for the name, percent four d for the quantity, percent nine point two f for the amount. Same widths on every line, and the columns line up. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** %-12s %4d %9.2f
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 11

### EP 11 S2 - printf in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 3–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Every part of %-8.2f, explained."
- **Script:** Percent minus eight point two f. Percent: start. Minus: left-align. Eight: width, at least eight spaces. Point two: two digits after the decimal. f: it's a float. Want zeros instead of spaces? Percent zero five d. Want a real percent sign? Percent percent. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 12 S2 - What does & mean?
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Why does scanf need this & sign?"
- **Script:** Why does scanf need the ampersand? Every variable lives at an address in memory, like a house number. Ampersand age means "the address of age". printf only needs the value. But scanf has to deliver a value into your box, so it needs the address. No address, no delivery. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 12

- **Script:** One missing character crashed my program. scanf, percent d, age. No ampersand. scanf used the junk inside age as an address and wrote into random memory. Crash. ## EP 13 - Type Casting

### EP 13 S1 - C says 5 / 2 = 2
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Calculator: 2.5. C: 2."
- **Script:** Calculator says five divided by two is two point five. C says two. Why? Both numbers are ints, so C gives an int answer and throws away the fraction. Five coconuts, two crabs: two each, one left over. And that leftover? Five percent two equals one. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 13 S2 - Casting in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "How to get 2.5 out of two ints."
- **Script:** How do you get two point five out of two ints? Cast one of them. Bracket float a, divided by b. The cast treats a as a float, so the division becomes a float division. Casting the other way, bracket int ninety-nine point nine nine, gives ninety-nine. It cuts, it does not round. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** (float)a / b · (int)99.99 = 99

### EP 13 S3 - Who stole 50 paise?

- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Split Rs 7 between two. C says Rs 3 each."
- **Script:** Split seven rupees between two friends. C says three each. Who stole fifty paise? total divided by people is int divided by int, so you get three, even if you store it in a float. Fix: bracket float total, divided by people. Three point five zero. Cast before you divide. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 65 - Stack vs Heap

### EP 65 S1 - Your room vs a godown
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 3 and 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A shelf collapses under rice sacks: "This is a stack overflow."
- **Script:** Your C program has two places for data. The stack is your room: small, fast, and cleaned automatically when a function ends. The heap is a rented godown: big, and yours until you hand back the key. Locals go on the stack. malloc gives you heap memory. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** STACK = ROOM · HEAP = GODOWN
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 65
- **Instagram caption:** The easiest way to remember it. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #memory #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Your room vs a godown | C Programming EP 65 - The easiest way to remember it. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #memory #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 65 S2 - Stack vs heap in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Two columns slam in: STACK and HEAP.
- **Script:** Stack: small, about one megabyte on Windows, very fast, cleaned automatically, dies with the function. Heap: big, slightly slower, you clean it with free, lives until you free it. Small and short-lived? Stack. Big, or needs to outlive the function? Heap. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** SMALL → STACK · BIG → HEAP
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 65
- **Instagram caption:** Screenshot this for your exam. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #memory #kodingkonkani #codingtips #stackvsheap
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Stack vs heap in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 65 - Screenshot this for your exam. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #memory #kodingkonkani #codingtips #stackvsheap #shorts

### EP 65 S3 - The real stack overflow
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A stack column bursting through its top.
- **Script:** int big of ten million, inside main. 40 megabytes. The Windows stack is 1 megabyte. No warning from GCC, but the program dies instantly. That's a real stack overflow. Fix: malloc it on the heap, check for NULL, and free it at the end. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** 40 MB DOES NOT FIT IN 1 MB
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 65
- **Instagram caption:** Now you know where the website got its name. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #debugging #kodingkonkani #stackoverflow #cbugs

## EP 66 - malloc & free

### EP 66 S1 - Arrays of any size
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "How many students?" with a blinking cursor: "You don't know yet. That's fine."
- **Script:** A normal array needs its size when you write the code. But what if the user decides? malloc of n times sizeof int asks the heap for exactly that many bytes, and gives you the address. Use it like a normal array. Then free it. Like hiring chairs for a wedding. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** SIZE DECIDED AT RUN TIME
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 66

### EP 66 S2 - The 4-step malloc habit
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4 and 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Four mint ticks stamping one after another.
- **Script:** Four steps, every single time. One: malloc with sizeof. Two: check for NULL. Three: use the memory. Four: free it, and set the pointer to NULL. Why the last part? Because free returns the memory, but your pointer still points there. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** MALLOC · CHECK · USE · FREE
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 66

### EP 66 S3 - The silent leak
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Memory meter racing up to 400 MB.
- **Script:** This function mallocs 4000 bytes and forgets to free them. When it returns, the pointer is gone, but the memory is not. Call it one lakh times: about 400 megabytes lost. No warning, and the program still prints Done. One line fixes it: free. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** NO free = MEMORY LEAK
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 66

### EP 66 S4 - Best friends

## EP 67 - calloc & realloc

### EP 67 S1 - Your array can grow
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A fish basket overflowing: "Out of space? Get a bigger basket."
- **Script:** Normal C arrays can never change size. But heap memory can. realloc takes your old block and a new size. If there's room, it stretches the block. If not, it moves your data to a bigger block and gives you the new address. Your fish are never lost. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** realloc = BIGGER BASKET
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 67
- **Instagram caption:** Arrays that grow. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #realloc #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Your array can grow | C Programming EP 67 - Arrays that grow. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #realloc #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 67 S2 - malloc vs calloc in 30s
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Two blocks: "? ? ?" and "0 0 0".
- **Script:** malloc gives you memory but doesn't clean it, so it holds garbage until you fill it. calloc takes a count and a size, and sets every byte to zero. Need counters or totals that start at zero? Use calloc. Filling everything yourself right away? malloc is fine. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** malloc = DIRTY · calloc = CLEAN
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 67
- **Instagram caption:** Know the difference for your viva. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #calloc #kodingkonkani #codingtips #malloc
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** malloc vs calloc in 30s | C Programming EP 67 - Know the difference for your viva. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #calloc #kodingkonkani #codingtips #malloc #shorts

### EP 67 S3 - The realloc trap
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `basket = realloc(basket, ...)` with a red underline.
- **Script:** basket equals realloc of basket. Looks fine, compiles fine. But if realloc fails, it returns NULL, and you just overwrote your only pointer to your data. Gone, and leaked. Always store the result in a temporary pointer first, check it, and only then update basket. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** NEVER p = realloc(p, ...)
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 67
- **Instagram caption:** One line, all your data gone. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #debugging #kodingkonkani #realloc #cbugs
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** The realloc trap | C Programming EP 67 - One line, all your data gone. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #debugging #kodingkonkani #realloc #cbugs #shorts

## EP 68 - Memory Bugs

### EP 68 S1 - The ghost pointer
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1 and 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A dotted ghost arrow pointing at a grey box: "This pointer is dead. It doesn't know."
- **Script:** You call free on a pointer. The memory goes back to the heap. But your pointer still holds the old address. It's a ghost, pointing at a room you've already returned. Read through it, and you get garbage, or worse. Fix: right after free, set the pointer to NULL. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** free() DOES NOT CLEAR YOUR POINTER
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 68
- **Instagram caption:** The scariest bug in C. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #learntocode #memorybugs
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** The ghost pointer | C Programming EP 68 - The scariest bug in C. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #learntocode #memorybugs #shorts

### EP 68 S2 - 3 memory bugs in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Three ghosts line up: LEAK, DANGLING, DOUBLE FREE.
- **Script:** Leak: you forgot to free, and lost the address. Dangling pointer: you freed it but kept using the old address. Double free: you freed the same block twice. The one habit that helps with two of them: free, then set the pointer to NULL. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** LEAK · DANGLING · DOUBLE FREE
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 68
- **Instagram caption:** Know your ghosts. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #memory #kodingkonkani #codingtips #memorybugs
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** 3 memory bugs in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 68 - Know your ghosts. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #memory #kodingkonkani #codingtips #memorybugs #shorts

### EP 68 S3 - double free detected
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "free(): double free detected in tcache 2 / Aborted".
- **Script:** free p. Then free p again. GCC 13 warns: pointer used after free. Run it on Linux and the program is killed: double free detected. Fix it with one line after the first free: p equals NULL. Freeing NULL is completely safe, it does nothing. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** free(NULL) IS SAFE
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 68

### EP 69 S1 - Why Did My Score Reset to 0?
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "score = 950" turning into "score = 0"
- **Script:** You score 950, close the program, open it again: 0. Variables live in RAM, and RAM is wiped when the program ends. To keep data, write it to a file on the disk. That's file handling in C. Full episode on Koding Konkani. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 69 S2 - Writing a File in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 5–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A file icon with text typing into it
- **Script:** Three steps. Open: FILE star fp equals fopen, file name, "w". Check: if fp is NULL, stop. Write: fprintf, fp, then your text, just like printf. Then close with fclose. "w" wipes the file first, "a" adds to the end. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

- **Script:** This program crashes before it prints anything. Why? The folder "notes" doesn't exist, so fopen returns NULL. Then fprintf writes to NULL. Crash. Always check: if fp equals NULL, use perror to print the reason, and return 1. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

### EP 71 S2 - fwrite and fread in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Three record cards flying into a drawer
- **Script:** fwrite saves raw bytes: where the data is, size of one item, how many, and the file. One call can save a whole array of structs. fread is its twin and loads them back. Both return how many items they handled, so loop while fread returns 1. Open with "wb" and "rb". **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 71 S3 - Windows Ate My Data
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Read 1 of 3 numbers" in Tomato
- **Script:** Saved three numbers, read back one. Why? I opened a binary file with "w" and "r". On Windows, text mode turns byte 10 into two bytes, and treats byte 26 as end of file. My numbers 10 and 26 broke it. Always use "wb" and "rb" for binary data. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

### EP 72 S1 - Same job, 1 second vs 1 year

### EP 72 S2 - Big-O in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 5 and 7
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Four shapes. That's all Big-O is."
- **Script:** Big-O describes how the steps grow when the input grows. O(1): same steps no matter what. O(log n): halve the problem each time, so a million items need about twenty steps. O(n): one step per item. O(n²): a loop inside a loop. Ten items, a hundred steps. A thousand items, a million steps. Multiply n by ten, the work multiplies by a hundred. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** O(1) · O(log n) · O(n) · O(n²)
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 72

Call the length function once, store it, and compare with that. Same trap with strlen. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 73 - Linear vs binary search, visualised

### EP 73 S1 - Any number in 7 guesses
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scene 1
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Think of a number from 1 to 100."
- **Script:** Think of a number from 1 to 100. 50? Higher. 75? Lower. 62, 68, 71, 73. Got it. I never need more than seven guesses, for any number. The trick has a name: binary search. And it's how computers search sorted data. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 1–100 IN 7 GUESSES
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 73

### EP 73 S2 - Binary search in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4 and 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Throw away half the array. Every step."
- **Script:** Binary search needs a sorted array. Look at the middle. If it's your target, done. If it's too small, the target can only be on the right, so throw away the whole left half. Too big? Throw away the right half. Repeat. A thousand items: about ten steps. A million: about twenty. That's O(log n). **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** HALVE IT. AGAIN. AGAIN.
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 73

### EP 73 S3 - The bracket that froze my program
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 10

- **Script:** mid equals low plus high divided by two. Looks fine? Division happens first, so it's low plus half of high. Search for 21 and mid gets stuck on index 5 forever. The program just hangs. Fix: low plus, in brackets, high minus low, divided by two. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** low + (high - low) / 2

## EP 74 - Bubble sort

### EP 74 S1 - The heaviest bubble
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–2
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Watch the biggest bubble."
- **Script:** In a glass of soda, the biggest bubbles reach the top first. There's a sorting algorithm that works exactly like that: on every pass, the biggest number floats to the end. It's called bubble sort, and we animate every single swap. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** BIGGEST FLOATS UP
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 74

- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** The heaviest bubble | C Programming EP 74 - Sorting, but make it soda. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #bubblesort #sorting #shorts

### EP 74 S2 - Bubble sort in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Compare. Swap. Repeat."
- **Script:** Bubble sort compares neighbours. If the left is bigger, swap. Walk to the end and the biggest value is now last. Lock it. Repeat on the rest. If a whole pass makes no swaps, the array is sorted, so stop early. Average and worst case: O(n squared). Best case with that early stop: O(n). **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** COMPARE · SWAP · REPEAT
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 74
- **Instagram caption:** Bubble sort, every swap animated. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #bubblesort #dsa
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Bubble sort in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 74 - Bubble sort, every swap animated. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #bubblesort #dsa #shorts

### EP 74 S3 - Where did my 9 go?
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 11
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "I sorted six numbers and one disappeared."
- **Script:** The inner loop says j less than n minus pass. Forgot the minus 1. So j reaches the last index and compares it with the slot after the array. Our 9 vanished and a minus 1 from outside the data appeared. GCC said nothing. Fix: j less than n minus 1 minus pass. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** j < n - 1 - pass
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 74

### EP 75 S2 - Selection sort in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4 and 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Find the smallest. Swap once. Repeat."
- **Script:** Selection sort scans the unsorted part and remembers where the smallest value is. Then it makes one swap to put it at the front. The sorted part grows by one each round. It always does about n squared over 2 comparisons, so it's O(n squared) every time, but it never makes more than n minus 1 swaps. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** ONE SWAP PER ROUND
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 75

### EP 76 S2 - Insertion sort in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4 and 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Lift. Shift. Drop."
- **Script:** Insertion sort keeps a sorted part on the left. Lift the next item, the key. Shift every bigger item one step right. Drop the key into the gap. Worst case O(n squared). But on data that's already nearly sorted, it's close to O(n), which is why real libraries use it for small or nearly sorted pieces. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** LIFT · SHIFT · DROP

- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 5 and 7
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Don't solve it. Trust the smaller call."
- **Script:** To move 3 discs: move the top 2 out of the way, move the biggest, then move the 2 back on top. How do you move 2? The same way, with 1. That's recursion: a function calling itself on a smaller problem, down to a base case. Each call gets its own frame on the call stack, and they finish in reverse order. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** TRUST THE SMALLER CALL
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 77

- **Script:** Remove the base case from hanoi and n goes 3, 2, 1, 0, minus 1, minus 2, forever. Each call stacks a new frame until memory runs out. ## EP 78 - Merge sort

### EP 78 S1 - Split the class
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–2
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "The laziest teacher has the fastest method."
- **Script:** The teacher splits the class in two and says, sort yourselves. Each half splits again, and again. Then she merges the sorted lines back together. That's merge sort, and it sorts a million numbers in about 20 million steps. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 78 S2 - Merge sort in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scenes 4, 5 and 11
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Split until it's trivial. Then zip it back up."
- **Script:** Merge sort cuts the array in half, again and again, until every piece is one number. One number is already sorted. Then it merges pairs of sorted pieces by comparing just their front items. log n levels of splitting, n work per level: O(n log n) in every case. The cost: an extra temp array. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** O(n log n) ALWAYS
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 78

### EP 79 S2 - Quick sort in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scenes 4 and 11
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Smaller left. Bigger right. Repeat."
- **Script:** Quick sort picks a pivot. It walks the array and swaps every smaller element into a zone on the left. Then the pivot moves in right after the zone, into its final place. Recurse on both sides. Good pivots give O(n log n) and no extra array. Bad pivots, like the last element of sorted data, give O(n squared). **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 79 S3 - p instead of p - 1
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 12
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Two characters missing. Infinite recursion."
- **Script:** quick\_sort a, low, p. Looks fine. But it includes the pivot in the left part. If the pivot ends up at the end, the next call is exactly the same as this one, forever, until the stack overflows. Exclude the pivot: p minus 1 and p plus 1. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 80 - Counting sort

### EP 80 S1 - A sort that never compares
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "This sort never compares two numbers."
- **Script:** The fish market auction man doesn't compare fish two at a time. He tallies them: four mackerel, two pomfret. Then reads the tally in order. That's counting sort, and for small ranges like exam marks, it's faster than any comparison sort. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** ZERO COMPARISONS
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 80

### EP 80 S2 - Counting sort in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4, 5 and 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Count. Then write."
- **Script:** Make one bucket per possible value. Pass over the array and add one to each value's bucket. Then walk the buckets in order and write each value as many times as it was counted. Time: O(n plus k), where k is the range. Brilliant for marks 0 to 100. Useless for phone numbers. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** O(n + k)
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 80

### EP 80 S3 - Minus 2 vanished
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 11
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "I sorted six temperatures and got five."
- **Script:** count of temps i, plus plus. For minus 2, that's count of minus 2, before the start of the array. C doesn't check, so it writes into someone else's memory and the value disappears. Fix: subtract the minimum, so minus 5 maps to bucket 0. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** INDEX = VALUE - MIN
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 80

## EP 82 - Build your own header library (sort.h + sort.c)

### EP 82 S1 - Make your own #include
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–2
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "stdio.h was written by someone. Write yours."
- **Script:** Every C program starts with hash include stdio dot h. Someone wrote that file. Today you write your own: sort dot h. Then any program can include it and get your sorting functions for free. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** #include "sort.h"
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 82
- **Instagram caption:** Your own C library in 7 minutes. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #headerfiles #cprojects
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Make your own #include | C Programming EP 82 - Your own C library in 7 minutes. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #headerfiles #cprojects #shorts

### EP 82 S2 - .h vs .c in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 3 and 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "The menu and the kitchen."
- **Script:** A header file is the menu: function names and parameters, no bodies. The .c file is the kitchen: the actual code. Hash include is just copy-paste of the menu into your file. And the include guard, ifndef, define, endif, makes sure the menu is pasted only once, even if two files ask for it. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** .h = MENU · .c = KITCHEN
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 82
- **Instagram caption:** Header files finally explained. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #headerfiles #includeguards
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** .h vs .c in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 82 - Header files finally explained. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #headerfiles #includeguards #shorts

### EP 82 S3 - Conflicting types for SortStats
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Identical code. Conflicting types?"
- **Script:** main.c includes sort.h, and so does report.h. Without an include guard, sort.h gets pasted twice. Each paste of the typedef struct makes a new type, so GCC says conflicting types for SortStats. Add ifndef SORT\_H, define SORT\_H, and endif. Every header, every time. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** ALWAYS ADD INCLUDE GUARDS
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 82
- **Instagram caption:** Three lines that save your build. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #bugoftheday #headerfiles
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Conflicting types for SortStats | C Programming EP 82 - Three lines that save your build. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #bugoftheday #headerfiles #shorts

### EP 82 S4 - Kept including itself

### EP 83 S2 - Stack and queue in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scenes 4 and 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Last in, first out. First in, first out."
- **Script:** A stack is an array plus a top index. Push: increase top, store. Pop: read, decrease top. A queue adds at the back and removes from the front. Use front plus count, modulo the size, and it wraps around like a ring. Every operation is O(1). Just check for full and empty first. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** LIFO · FIFO · O(1)
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 83

### EP 83 S3 - Popped a number nobody pushed
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 11
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "I popped a 0. I never pushed a 0."
- **Script:** A stack of size 3 with no checks. Push four values: the fourth lands outside the array. Pop five times: the last pop reads stack of minus 1 and gives garbage, and top ends at minus 2. Always check for full before push and empty before pop. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

### EP 85 S2 - Input Validation in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "abc", "-50" and "0" bouncing off a shield
- **Script:** Two checks make input safe. One: did scanf return 1? If not, the user typed something that isn't a number. Two: is the value in range, like greater than zero? If either fails, clear the line and ask again. Put this in one function, readAmount, and call it everywhere. Write the checking once, use it everywhere. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 85 S3 - The Bug That Robs the Bank
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** balance counter spinning to "Rs -40000.00"
- **Script:** This withdraw code reads the amount and subtracts it. No checks. Minus 5,000 adds money. 50,000 sends you negative. Letters give garbage. The fix: gate one, reject anything that isn't a positive number. Gate two, decline if it would drop below the minimum balance. Only then touch the balance. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 15 - Arithmetic operators

### EP 15 S1 - Is today an even day?
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–2
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Is today an even day? C knows in one symbol."
- **Script:** Is today an even day? C knows in one symbol. Today is the thirtieth. Thirty percent two equals zero, so it's even. That percent sign is the remainder operator, and it's one of the most useful tools in C. Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 30 % 2 = 0
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 15

### EP 15 S2 - / vs % in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 5 and 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "17 divided by 5 is 3 in C. Here's where the 2 went."
- **Script:** 17 divided by 5 is 3 in C. Here's where the 2 went. Put 17 fish into baskets of 5. Three full baskets: that's 17 slash 5. Two fish left over: that's 17 percent 5. Slash gives full groups, percent gives what's left. Use it for minutes to hours: 135 slash 60 is 2 hours, 135 percent 60 is 15 minutes. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** / = GROUPS · % = LEFTOVER
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 15

- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "This one symbol breaks on decimals."
- **Script:** This one symbol breaks on decimals. kg percent 2, where kg is a float. GCC says: invalid operands to binary percent, have float and int. Percent only works on whole numbers. For decimals, include math dot h and use fmod: fmod of kg comma 2. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "b = a++ and d = ++c. Watch the boxes."
- **Script:** b equals a plus plus, and d equals plus plus c. Watch the boxes. a is 5. Post-increment: use first, so b gets 5, then a becomes 6. c is 5. Pre-increment: add first, so c becomes 6, then d gets 6. Read left to right: whatever you see first happens first. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 18 S1 - Your computer sees switches
- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scenes 1–2

- **Script:** You see 12. Your computer sees four switches: on, on, off, off. 1 1 0 0. Every number is just switches. And C has operators that flip them one at a time. They're called bitwise operators. Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 18 S2 - & | ^ in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "12 AND 10 is 8. Here's why."
- **Script:** 12 AND 10 is 8. Here's why. 12 is 1 1 0 0. 10 is 1 0 1 0. Compare column by column. AND keeps a 1 only where both are 1: 1 0 0 0, which is 8. OR keeps a 1 where either is 1: 1 1 1 0, 14. XOR keeps a 1 where they differ: 0 1 1 0, 6. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** & BOTH · | EITHER · ^ DIFFERENT

- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 9

- **Script:** Both are true. C says false. hasTicket is 2 and hasSeat is 1, both non-zero, both true. But we wrote a single ampersand. That's bitwise AND: 1 0 and 0 1 share no bits, so the answer is 0. Use double ampersand for true-or-false questions. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

### EP 20 S2 - if/else in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Code line 9 glows: "if (marks >= 35)"
- **Script:** if and else in 30 seconds. Write if, then a condition in round brackets. If it is true, C runs the block in curly braces. If it is false, C jumps to else and runs that block instead. Code after both blocks runs every time. Type 72: Pass. Type 20: Fail. Type 35? Greater than or equal, so Pass. Always test the edge value. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** TRUE = IF BLOCK · FALSE = ELSE BLOCK

### EP 20 S3 - The semicolon that lies
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal shows "Pass!" next to "marks = 20". Xecxec's jaw drops.
- **Script:** 20 marks. The program says Pass. What? Look closely. There is a semicolon right after the if. In C, a lone semicolon is an empty statement. So the if controls nothing, and the block below runs every time. Plain gcc does not even complain. Compile with -Wall and it warns you. Fix: delete that semicolon. Always compile with -Wall. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** if (...); IS A TRAP

## EP 21 - else-if ladders

### EP 21 S1 - Five grades, one ladder
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Report card slams down: "A? B? C? D? ...CRAB?"
- **Script:** if and else give you two roads. Your report card needs five. So C gives you a ladder. Each rung asks one question. 90 or more? Grade A. 75 or more? Grade B. And so on, down to the bottom. The moment one rung says yes, you get off. That is the else-if ladder. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 5 GRADES, 1 LADDER
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 21

### EP 21 S2 - First true wins
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4 and 7
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "80" slides down a ladder and stops at B.
- **Script:** else-if in 30 seconds. C checks each condition from the top. 80: is it 90 or more? No. 75 or more? Yes. Print Grade B, and stop. It never checks 60 or 35, even though those are true too. First true wins. And because of that, you never need to write "and less than 90". The rung above already handled it. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** FIRST TRUE WINS

### EP 21 S3 - Topper gets a D
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "marks = 95" then "Grade D". Xecxec faints.
- **Script:** 95 marks. Grade D. No error. No warning. What happened? The first check was 35 or more. 95 is more than 35, so it is caught right there. The 90 check never runs, for anyone, ever. Fix: put the strictest check first. 90, then 75, then 60, then 35. In an else-if ladder, order is everything. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 22 - Nested if

### EP 22 S1 - The bouncer's two questions
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Xecxec in sunglasses blocks the gate: "Ticket?"
- **Script:** A bouncer at a beach concert asks two questions. Ticket? Then ID. But he never asks for ID if you have no ticket. The second question lives inside the first. In C, that is a nested if: an if inside the block of another if. Three paths, one gate. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 22 S2 - Nested if in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Code with outer block tinted dark blue, inner block mint
- **Script:** Nested if in 30 seconds. The outer if checks the ticket. Only if that is true do we enter its block, ask for age, and reach the inner if. Inner true: welcome. Inner false: adults only. Outer false: we skip everything inside, and go to the outer else. No ticket, no entry. Indent each level so your eyes can follow it. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 22 S3 - The dangling else
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal shows an empty output. Xecxec taps the screen. "Hello?"
- **Script:** This code should print no ticket, no entry. It prints nothing. The else is lined up with the outer if, but C ignores spaces. An else always belongs to the nearest if without an else. That is the inner if. So the whole thing is skipped. Compile with -Wall and GCC warns: suggest explicit braces to avoid ambiguous else. Add braces, and it works. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** ELSE GOES TO THE NEAREST IF
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 22

## EP 23 - switch-case

### EP 23 S1 - The menu machine
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Order bell rings, chalkboard menu: "1, 2 or 3?"
- **Script:** A customer says a number. The kitchen makes that dish. You could write a long else-if ladder. Or you could use switch. Drop one value in, and C jumps straight to the matching case. No match? It goes to default. One value in, one exact match, one exit. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 23 S2 - switch in 30 seconds

- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Arrow jumps from "switch (choice)" straight to "case 2:"
- **Script:** switch in 30 seconds. Write switch and the value in brackets. Each case is a label with a colon. C jumps straight to the matching case and runs from there. break means leave the switch now. default catches anything that does not match. It only works with whole numbers and characters, and each case must be a constant. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

- **Script:** Order number 1. The bill shows fish thali, prawn curry, bebinca, and not on the menu. Four lines! Someone forgot the breaks. A case is where C starts, not where it stops. ## EP 24 - Logic puzzles in C

### EP 24 S1 - Xecxec's driftwood riddle
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Three driftwood sticks (1, 2, 10) drop on sand and fail to meet. "Triangle?"
- **Script:** Three sticks: 1, 2 and 10. Can they make a triangle? No. Any two sides together must be longer than the third. Your program can check that with one line of C: a plus b less than or equal to c, OR b plus c less than or equal to a, OR a plus c less than or equal to b. If any is true, it is not a triangle. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** TRIANGLE OR NOT?
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 24

### EP 24 S2 - AND vs OR in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3–4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Two cards: "ALL of these" and "ANY of these"
- **Script:** Turning words into conditions in 30 seconds. "All of these are true" becomes AND. "Any one of these is true" becomes OR. And C is lazy in a good way. With OR, the moment one part is true, it stops checking. With AND, the moment one part is false, it stops. That is called short-circuit evaluation. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** ALL = && · ANY = ||
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 24
- **Instagram caption:** The one trick for writing any condition in C. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #ctutorial #programming

### EP 24 S3 - a == b == c lies
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** a = 2, b = 2, c = 2. Output: "Not equilateral". Xecxec drops his magnifying glass.
- **Script:** All three are 2. The program says not equal. Why? C reads a equals b equals c left to right. a equals b is true, and true is 1. Then it checks: is 1 equal to c? c is 2. False. With -Wall, GCC warns: suggest parentheses around comparison. Fix: a equals b AND b equals c. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** a == b == c IS NOT MATHS
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 24

### EP 25 S5 - The mystery space
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 10–11
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `scanf(" %c")` with the space circled in red: "Why is this here?"
- **Script:** Quiz. Why is there a space before percent c? Three, two, one. When you press Enter, a hidden newline character stays behind. Without the space, the next percent c reads that newline and skips your question. The space tells scanf to skip whitespace first. Now your challenge: add three more questions and score out of 5. ## EP 28 - do-while: Ask First, Check Later

### EP 28 S1 - The User Typed 150 as Their Age

- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Age: 150" in Tomato with a shocked Xecxec
- **Script:** Your program asks for age. The user types 150. Then zero. You need to keep asking until the answer makes sense, and you must ask at least once first. That's the job of the do-while loop. It runs, then checks. Full episode on Koding Konkani. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 28 S2 - while vs do-while in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Guard at a door vs an open door
- **Script:** x is 10. while x is less than 5: the check happens first, it's false, the body runs zero times. do-while x is less than 5: the body runs first, prints once, then the check fails. while checks at the door. do-while lets you in, then checks. That's the only difference. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

- **Length / source:** 0:20 · built from scene 8

### EP 29 S2 - break vs continue in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3–4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** EXIT door vs SKIP arrow
- **Script:** break and continue. break leaves the loop completely and jumps past the closing brace. continue skips only the rest of this round and moves to the next one. Exit versus skip. And both only affect the innermost loop. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Found it!" then more output keeps printing
- **Script:** We found the cell and called break. But the program kept searching row 3. Why? break only leaves the loop it's directly inside, the inner one. The outer loop carried on. ### EP 32 S2 - The Pyramid Formula in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Row table appearing beside the pyramid
- **Script:** Every pyramid follows two rules. Spaces on row i: n minus i. Stars on row i: 2i minus 1. For n equals 5, that's 4 spaces and 1 star, then 3 and 3, then 2 and 5, then 1 and 7, then 0 and 9. Write those two rules as loop limits, and you're done. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** SPACES = n - i / STARS = 2i - 1

- **Script:** This should be a pyramid. It's a lopsided triangle. The stars loop says k up to i, so stars grow by 1. But spaces shrink by 1, so stars must grow by 2 to stay centred. Change it to 2i minus 1. Fixed. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 7
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** 1234 turning into 4321
- **Script:** Reverse 1234 with two tricks. Mod 10 gives the last digit: 4. Divide by 10 removes it: 123. Build the answer with reversed times 10 plus digit. 4, then 43, then 432, then 4321. Loop while the number is more than zero. Done. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

## EP 34 - What Are Functions?

### EP 34 S1 - Stop Copy-Pasting Your Code
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** the code panel scrolling endlessly with paste sounds
- **Script:** Print dashes. Print a greeting. Dashes again. Greeting again. Copy, paste, copy, paste. Now change the greeting in ten places. Or don't. Write it once as a function, give it a name, and call that name whenever you need it. Change it in one place, and every call updates. That's functions in C, and it starts in episode 34. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** STOP COPY-PASTING · WRITE IT ONCE

### EP 34 S2 - Functions in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `void greet(void)` with four tags popping on
- **Script:** A function in C has four parts. Return type: what it gives back. void means nothing. Name: greet. Brackets: the inputs. void means none. Body: the code in curly braces. Defining it teaches C the recipe. Calling it, greet with empty brackets and a semicolon, actually runs it. Define once, call many times. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** RETURN TYPE · NAME · INPUTS · BODY

### EP 34 S3 - Bug: Implicit Declaration
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** red error text "implicit declaration of function 'greet'"
- **Script:** You call greet in main, and GCC says: implicit declaration of function greet. Why? Because greet is written below main. C reads top to bottom, so at that line it has never met greet. Move the function above main, and it compiles. Next time GCC says "implicit declaration", it means: introduce your function first. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** "WHO IS greet?" — GCC
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 34
- **Instagram caption:** The most common first-function error in C, fixed. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #debugging #kodingkonkani #gcc #learntocode

### EP 35 S1 - A Function Is a Juice Machine
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3

- **Script:** Mango in, juice out. 7 in, 49 out. That's a C function with a parameter and a return value. The parameter is the empty glass waiting inside the function. The argument is what you pour in when you call it. And return hands the answer back. **Follow for the next one.**

- **Script:** In int square, int n, the n is a parameter: a variable inside the function. In square of 7, the 7 is an argument: the actual value you send. ### EP 35 S3 - The Missing Return Bug
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** output "Bigger: 3" with a big tomato question mark
- **Script:** Bigger of 3 and 9 should be 9. This program printed 3. Why? The function returns a only when a is bigger. Otherwise it falls off the end and returns garbage. GCC even warned: control reaches end of non-void function. Add return b at the end, and you get 9. Every path in a non-void function needs a return. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** BIGGER OF 3 AND 9 = 3??

## EP 36 - Prototypes & Header Files

### EP 36 S1 - Introduce Yourself First
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** a stranger asking "Give me your notes"
- **Script:** A stranger asks for your notes. Who are you? Same with C. Call a function the compiler hasn't met, and it complains. The fix is a prototype: one line at the top, like int cube, int n, semicolon. Name, inputs, output. Now the compiler knows who cube is, and you can write the body anywhere below. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** NAME TAG FOR YOUR FUNCTION
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 36

- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Introduce Yourself First | C Programming EP 36 - A prototype is your function's name tag. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #functions #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 36 S2 - What's Inside stdio.h?
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `#include <stdio.h>` cracking open like a box
- **Script:** You've typed include stdio dot h a hundred times. What's inside? Mostly prototypes. One of them introduces printf. The include line copies that file's text into your program, so the compiler meets printf before you use it. A dot h file is a list of introductions. A dot c file has the real code. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** .h = MENU · .c = KITCHEN

## EP 37 - Scope & Lifetime

### EP 37 S1 - What Happens in a Function Stays There
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scene 1 and 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** tomato error "'basket' undeclared"
- **Script:** We made basket inside a function. main tries to print it. Error: basket undeclared. Why? A local variable only exists inside its own function. It is born when the function is called and destroyed when it returns. What happens in a function stays in the function. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** LOCAL = STAYS INSIDE
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 37

### EP 37 S2 - static in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 5–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "visits = 1, 2, 3 but fresh = 1, 1, 1"
- **Script:** Two variables inside the same function. fresh is a normal local: it is reset to zero on every call. visits is static: it is set up once and keeps its value between calls. Call the function three times. visits counts 1, 2, 3. fresh is stuck at 1. Static means: same scope, longer memory. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** static = REMEMBERS

- **Script:** score starts at 10, the loop adds 5 three times, but it prints 10. The culprit: int score inside the loop. That creates a second score that hides the first. ## EP 38 - Call by Value

### EP 38 S1 - Why My Swap Didn't Swap
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1 and 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "Inside: swapped. Outside: not swapped."
- **Script:** Inside swap, a is 9 and b is 5. Swapped. Back in main, x is still 5 and y is still 9. The function swapped perfectly, just not your variables. C handed it copies. It swapped the copies, then threw them away. That's call by value. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** SWAPPED... THE COPIES
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 38

### EP 38 S2 - Call by Value in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3 and 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** a photocopier spitting out a sheet with `5` on it
- **Script:** When you call a function in C, it gets a photocopy of each argument. It can scribble on the copy all it wants. Your original variable stays the same. When the function ends, the copies are thrown away. Want a result back? Use return. Want to change the original? You'll need its address. That's pointers. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** FUNCTIONS GET PHOTOCOPIES
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 38

## EP 39 - Recursion

### EP 39 S1 - A Function That Calls Itself
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** a long bus-stand queue whispering "What's your position?"
- **Script:** You're in a queue and want your position. Ask the person ahead. They ask the person ahead. Until the first person says "I'm number one". Then the answers come back: two, three, four. That's recursion: a function that calls itself on a smaller problem, with a base case where it stops. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** RECURSION = ASK THE NEXT PERSON
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 39

### EP 39 S2 - The Call Stack in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** four plates stacking up labelled factorial(4) to factorial(1)
- **Script:** factorial of 4 calls factorial of 3, which calls 2, which calls 1. Each call is a plate on the stack, with its own n. factorial of 1 returns 1. Then the plates come off: 2 times 1 is 2, 3 times 2 is 6, 4 times 6 is 24. Going down, we pile up. Coming back, we multiply. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** PILE UP · THEN MULTIPLY
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 39

### EP 39 S3 - Stack Overflow Bug
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** frames piling up past the top of the screen, screen shaking
- **Script:** This factorial has no base case. It calls 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0, minus 1, and never stops. Every call takes stack space, until the stack runs out and the program crashes. A stack overflow. GCC even warns: infinite recursion detected. Fix: if n is less than or equal to 1, return 1. Base case first, always. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** NO BASE CASE = CRASH
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 39

- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** the call counter rolling up past 300,000,000
- **Script:** This tiny Fibonacci function looks innocent. Ask it for fib of 40, and it makes 331 million calls. Why? It calls itself twice, and the same values get worked out again and again. fib of 10: 177 calls. fib of 40: 331 million. The same answer with a loop takes 40 steps. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 40 S2 - The Recursion Recipe
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 3 and 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** recipe card "3 questions for every recursion"
- **Script:** Three questions solve any recursion problem. What's the smallest case? How is my problem a smaller copy? How do I combine? Sum of digits: if n is 0, return 0. Otherwise, the last digit, n mod 10, plus the sum of digits of n divided by 10. 4031 becomes 1 plus 3 plus 0 plus 4. 8. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** SMALLEST · SMALLER COPY · COMBINE

## EP 41 - Arrays: A Row of Numbered Lockers

### EP 41 S1 - 50 Variables? No Way
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Xecxec buried under 50 variable boxes. "Stop typing mark1, mark2, mark3..."
- **Script:** Stop typing mark1, mark2, mark3. If you need fifty marks, you need one array. An array is a row of lockers: one name, many values, each with its own number. int marks fifty, and you are done. Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 50 VARIABLES? → 1 ARRAY
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 41

### EP 41 S2 - Arrays in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "The first box is not number one."
- **Script:** The first box is not number one. In C, int marks five gives you five boxes, numbered zero to four. marks zero is the first, marks four is the last. To visit all of them, loop i from zero while i is less than five. Size five, last index four. Always size minus one. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** SIZE 5 = INDEX 0 TO 4
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 41

## EP 43 - Searching: Finding Your Name in the Register

### EP 43 S1 - 1000 Names, 10 Steps
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Giant register: "Find one name in 1000. In 10 steps."
- **Script:** Find one name in a thousand, in about ten steps. Impossible? Not if the list is sorted. Look at the middle. Too small? Throw away the left half. Too big? Throw away the right half. Repeat. That is binary search. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 1000 NAMES → 10 STEPS
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 43

### EP 43 S2 - Binary Search in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 5–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** low, mid, high arrows over eight boxes.
- **Script:** Three arrows: low, mid, high. mid is low plus high, divided by two. If the middle value is your target, done. If it is smaller, move low to mid plus one. If it is bigger, move high to mid minus one. Keep going while low is less than or equal to high. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** low · mid · high

- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "found at index -1" with 21 glowing at index 0.
- **Script:** 21 is right there at index zero, but binary search says not found. Why? The array is not sorted. The middle value was 12, 21 is bigger, so it threw away the left half, the half that had 21. Binary search only works on sorted data. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "142 150 138 155 0". "My tallest student vanished."
- **Script:** My tallest student vanished. The loop compared h i with h i plus one, but it ran while i was less than n. So on the last turn it peeked outside the array, swapped with whatever was there, and 160 was gone. If you use i plus one, stop at n minus one. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 46 - Arrays & Functions: Hand Over the Whole Locker Row

### EP 46 S1 - This Function Changed My Array!
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1, 6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Split screen: int stays 5, array marks all jump by 5.
- **Script:** Pass an int to a function, change it, and main does not see the change. Pass an array, change it, and main does see it. Same language, different rules. The reason is in the full episode. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** int: NO CHANGE · ARRAY: CHANGED
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 46

### EP 46 S2 - Count an Array in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "How many elements? Let C count."
- **Script:** Want C to count your array for you? sizeof marks gives the total bytes. sizeof marks zero gives the bytes of one element. Divide them, and you get the number of elements. Twenty bytes divided by four is five. Only works where the array is declared. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** sizeof(arr) / sizeof(arr\[0\])

### EP 46 S3 - sizeof Lied to Me
- **Length / source:** 0:40 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "Average: 85.00" next to the real answer 78.80.
- **Script:** My average says 85, but the real answer is 78.80. I used sizeof inside the function. There, the array is just an address, eight bytes on a 64-bit PC. Eight divided by four is two, so it averaged only two marks. GCC even warned me. Count in main, pass the size. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** sizeof INSIDE A FUNCTION = WRONG

## EP 47 - Strings Are Char Arrays: The Secret Character

### EP 47 S1 - 6 Letters, 7 Bytes?
- **Length / source:** 0:22 · built from scenes 1, 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "Panjim has 7 bytes". Xecxec counting six on his claws.
- **Script:** Panjim has six letters. So why does C say seven bytes? Because every string in C ends with a hidden character, backslash zero. It is the stop sign that tells printf where the string ends. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 6 LETTERS + \\0 = 7 BYTES
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 47
- **Instagram caption:** The secret character at the end of every C string. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #strings #codingforbeginners

### EP 47 S2 - C Strings in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A bus stopping at a \\0 stop sign.
- **Script:** C has no string type. A string is a char array that ends with backslash zero. printf with percent s starts at the first letter and stops at backslash zero. To loop through a string, keep going while the char is not backslash zero. Length n needs n plus one bytes. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** STRING = CHARS + \\0
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 47

- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "Hello, SaraKamat!". "I only printed first name."
- **Script:** I only printed the first name. Why did the surname come too? char first four equals Sara. Four letters, four boxes, no room for backslash zero. So printf never found the stop sign and kept reading into the next string. Leave room: use empty brackets. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 48 - Reading Strings: Why Your Full Name Got Cut

### EP 48 S1 - Where Did My Surname Go?
- **Length / source:** 0:22 · built from scenes 1, 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: typed "Atreya Kamat", printed "Hello, Atreya!"
- **Script:** I typed Atreya Kamat. C said Hello, Atreya. Where did Kamat go? scanf percent s stops at the first space. The rest is still waiting in the keyboard buffer. The fix is one function: fgets. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** "ATREYA KAMAT" → "ATREYA"
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 48
