- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Where Did My Surname Go? | C Programming EP 48 - scanf cut my name in half. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #strings #codingforbeginners #shorts

### EP 48 S2 - fgets in 30 Seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** fgets( \_\_\_, \_\_\_, \_\_\_ ) with three blanks.
- **Script:** fgets takes three things: where to store the text, how many bytes it may use, and where to read from. fgets, name, sizeof name, stdin. It reads the whole line, spaces included, and never overflows. One catch: it keeps the newline. Remove it with strcspn. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** fgets(name, sizeof(name), stdin);
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 48
- **Instagram caption:** The safe way to read a line in C. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #fgets #cprogrammingtutorial
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** fgets in 30 Seconds | C Programming EP 48 - The safe way to read a line in C. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #fgets #cprogrammingtutorial #shorts

### EP 48 S3 - Never Use gets()
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 7
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "gets(name);" stamped with a red NO.
- **Script:** If your book teaches gets, skip that page. gets has no size limit. Type more letters than the array holds, and it writes past the end, over other memory. That is a buffer overflow. gets was removed from C in C11. Use fgets. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** gets() = REMOVED FROM C
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 48
- **Instagram caption:** The function so dangerous C deleted it. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #bufferoverflow #codingsafety

- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3–4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Crossing arrows between "feni" and "inef".

### EP 52 S1 - Every variable has a house number
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1–3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Gate number zooms in: "Your variable lives HERE."
- **Script:** Every variable in C lives somewhere. Your RAM is a long street of tiny houses, one byte each, and every house has a number called an address. When you write int age equals 19, the computer picks four houses and puts 19 inside. Want to see the house number? That is one symbol away. Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**

### EP 52 S2 - & in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Big ampersand spins in: "This symbol = address of."
- **Script:** The ampersand in C means "address of". Ampersand age gives you the house number where age lives. To print it, use percent p, and put void star in brackets in front. An int uses four bytes, a char uses one, a double uses eight. The address is always the number of the first byte. That is the whole idea. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9

- **Script:** Printing an address with percent d? GCC warns you, and you get a weird negative number. On a 64-bit computer an address is eight bytes, but percent d reads only four. ## EP 53 - What Is a Pointer?

### EP 53 S1 - A pointer is a GPS pin
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 4

It holds the address of the value. int star p equals ampersand age means: p stores where age lives. We draw that as an arrow. That is a pointer. **Follow for the next one.**

- **Script:** int star p. Then star p equals 5. Looks fine? It is not. p was never given an address, so it holds garbage and points somewhere random. ## EP 55 - Call by Reference

### EP 55 S1 - Why your swap didn't swap
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "After: x = 5, y = 9" flashes red: "Your swap is lying to you."
- **Script:** You wrote a swap function, and nothing swapped. Here is why. C sends photocopies of x and y into the function. The function swaps the copies perfectly. Then the function ends, and the copies are thrown away. Your real x and y never moved. The fix is one idea: send the address. Full episode on the channel. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** C SENDS PHOTOCOPIES
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 55
- **Instagram caption:** The most famous beginner bug in C. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Why your swap didn't swap | C Programming EP 55 - The most famous beginner bug in C. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 55 S2 - Call by reference in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scenes 4–5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Two arrows cross from `a` and `b` into `x` and `y`.
- **Script:** To let a function change your variables, send their addresses. In the call: swap of ampersand x, ampersand y. In the function: int star a, int star b. Then temp equals star a, star a equals star b, star b equals temp. The function follows the arrows and changes the real boxes. x and y are swapped. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** \* IN THE HEADER · & IN THE CALL
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 55
- **Instagram caption:** Write this on your exam sheet. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #codingtips #callbyreference
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** Call by reference in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 55 - Write this on your exam sheet. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #codingtips #callbyreference #shorts

### EP 55 S3 - Forgot the ampersands
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `swap(x, y);` then the screen cracks.
- **Script:** Your swap takes pointers, but you called swap of x comma y. GCC warns: makes pointer from integer without a cast. Now a points to house number 5, which your program does not own. Crash. The fix: swap of ampersand x, ampersand y. Two characters. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** swap(&x, &y) NOT swap(x, y)
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 55

## EP 56 - Pointer Arithmetic

### EP 56 S1 - p++ does not add 1
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Address 1000, then `p++`, then 1004: "Wait, plus FOUR?"
- **Script:** p points to an int at address 1000. We do p plus plus. The new address is not 1001. It is 1004. Because an int is four bytes, and a pointer always steps one whole element. One house, not one plot. That is pointer arithmetic. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** p++ = NEXT ELEMENT
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 56

- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** p++ does not add 1 | C Programming EP 56 - Pointers walk in houses, not plots. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #pointers #kodingkonkani #learntocode #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 56 S2 - Pointer arithmetic in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3–4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Three arrows hopping at three speeds: char, int, double.
- **Script:** Add 1 to a char pointer, it moves 1 byte. To an int pointer, 4 bytes. To a double pointer, 8 bytes. The pointer always moves by the size of its type. And subtracting two pointers gives elements, not bytes. 12 bytes apart for ints means 3 elements. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** STEP = sizeof(TYPE)
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 56

### EP 56 S3 - The void pointer trap
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Output `1426063360`: "Where did this come from?"
- **Script:** void star v points to an int array. v plus plus. Then read it as an int. You get one billion four hundred million. Why? A void pointer has no size, so GCC moves just one byte, into the middle of an int. Standard C does not even allow this. Use a real type like int star. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** NO MATHS ON void\*
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 56

### EP 56 S5 - Quiz: what does p - first give?
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 5 and 10
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Two arrows on an int array, 12 bytes apart: "12 or 3?"

## EP 57 - Pointers & Arrays

### EP 57 S1 - Your array is lying to you
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `marks` and `&marks[0]` print the same address: "Same thing?!"
- **Script:** Print the array name marks with percent p. Now print ampersand marks of zero. Same address! When you use an array's name, C quietly turns it into a pointer to the first element. Like a locker row: know where locker 1 is, and you can find them all. **Follow for the next one.**

So marks square bracket 2 and star of marks plus 2 are exactly the same. Square brackets are pointer arithmetic in a nice outfit. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 57 S3 - Never return a local array
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A stack frame dissolves while an arrow still points into it.
- **Script:** A function makes an array and returns it. GCC warns: function returns address of local variable. When the function ends, its frame vanishes, and your pointer points into nothing. The program crashes. Fix: let the caller own the array and pass it in to be filled. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** LOCAL ARRAY DIES WITH ITS FUNCTION

- **Script:** A string is just chars in a row, ending with backslash zero. A char pointer starts at the first letter. Print star p, then p plus plus. Repeat until star p is backslash zero. ### EP 59 S1 - A pin to a pin
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3

- **Script:** pp holds the address of p. Star pp follows one arrow and gives p. Star star pp follows two arrows and gives the value. Each star is one hop. Count the stars, count the hops. And star star pp equals 50 changes the value at the end of the chain. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**

### EP 59 S3 - The NULL crash
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `*p` on a NULL pointer, then the screen cracks.
- **Script:** int star p equals NULL. Then print star p. No warning from GCC. But NULL points nowhere, so there's nothing to read. Segmentation fault. The fix is one habit: if p is not NULL, then use it. One check, a hundred crashes saved. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** CHECK NULL FIRST

### EP 60 S1 - You can point at a function?!

### EP 62 S2 - The arrow in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4 and 8
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** A GPS pin drops on an ID card: "p->marks"
- **Script:** The arrow in 30 seconds. p is a pointer to a struct. To reach its marks, you could write star p in brackets, dot marks. Brackets needed, because dot is stronger than star. Too ugly. So C gives a shortcut: p arrow marks. Same meaning. The rule: have the struct, use dot. Have a pointer, use arrow. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** p->x == (\*p).x
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 62
- **Instagram caption:** Dot vs arrow, settled forever. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #pointers #ctutorial

- **Script:** p is a pointer to a struct, and you wrote p dot marks. GCC says: p is a pointer, did you mean to use arrow? It even draws it for you. The reverse also fails: a real struct with an arrow gives invalid type argument of arrow. Fix: pointer, arrow. Struct, dot. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**

## EP 63 - typedef & enum

### EP 63 S1 - What does 2 mean?
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 4
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** `light = 2;` with a giant question mark. Xecxec squints.
- **Script:** light equals 2. Is that red? Green? Bus number 2? Mystery numbers make code impossible to read. An enum gives numbers names. RED is 0, YELLOW is 1, GREEN is 2. Now you write light equals GREEN. The computer still stores 2. But every human understands it. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** light = GREEN; NOT light = 2;
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 63

### EP 63 S2 - typedef in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 3 and 5
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "struct Student" gets a sticker: "Hi, I'm Student"
- **Script:** typedef in 30 seconds. It gives a type a nickname. typedef struct, the members in braces, then the short name, like Bus. Now you write Bus b, without the word struct. It does not make a new type, only a new name for it. Works with enums too: typedef enum, the values, then Signal. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** typedef = NICKNAME
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 63

### EP 63 S3 - Two REDs, one error
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Two RED tags crash into each other
- **Script:** enum Signal has RED. enum Fruit also has RED. GCC says: redeclaration of enumerator RED. In C, enum names do not live inside their enum. They all share one big room. Two things, one name, not allowed. Fix: prefixes. SIGNAL\_RED and FRUIT\_RED. Real C libraries do exactly this. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** redeclaration of enumerator 'RED'
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 63

## EP 64 - union & memory layout

### EP 64 S1 - 1 + 4 + 1 = 12?
- **Length / source:** 0:25 · built from scenes 1 and 3
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Terminal: "6 bytes" then "sizeof = 12". Record scratch.
- **Script:** A char, an int and a char. 1 plus 4 plus 1 is 6. sizeof says 12. Why? The processor likes ints to start at a multiple of 4. So C adds empty bytes, called padding: 3 after the first char, 3 at the end. Six bytes of data, six bytes of air. Your sizes may differ, so always use sizeof. **Follow for the next one.**
- **On-screen text:** 1 + 4 + 1 = 12?!
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 64
- **Instagram caption:** The hidden bytes inside your struct. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #memory #codingforbeginners
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** 1 + 4 + 1 = 12? | C Programming EP 64 - The hidden bytes inside your struct. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #memory #codingforbeginners #shorts

### EP 64 S2 - union in 30 seconds
- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 5–6
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** Three locker doors merge into one
- **Script:** union in 30 seconds. A struct gives each member its own locker. A union gives all members one shared locker, as big as the biggest member. Store an int, then a float, and the int is gone. Only the last one you wrote is valid. Use it when a value can be one of several types, but never two at once. **Subscribe, and learn C one episode at a time.**
- **On-screen text:** STRUCT: ALL · UNION: ONE AT A TIME
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 64
- **Instagram caption:** Struct vs union in one picture. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #union #ctutorial
- **YouTube Shorts title/caption:** union in 30 seconds | C Programming EP 64 - Struct vs union in one picture. Subscribe to Koding Konkani for the full episode. #cprogramming #learntocode #kodingkonkani #union #ctutorial #shorts

### EP 64 S3 - 2.5 became 1075838976
- **Length / source:** 0:35 · built from scene 9
- **Hook (0-1.5s):** "v.f = 2.5" then "v.i = 1075838976". Xecxec's eyes pop.
- **Script:** We stored 2.5 as a float in a union. Then we read it as an int. Output: over one billion. No error, no warning. The union has no idea what is inside. C just reads the float's bit pattern as a whole number. Fix: a tagged union. Keep an enum next to it that says what is stored. Check it before you read. **Follow Koding Konkani, so you never ship this bug.**
- **On-screen text:** WRONG MEMBER = WRONG NUMBER
- **End card (last 2s):** FOLLOW @YOUR_IG_HANDLE / SUBSCRIBE: Koding Konkani - Full video: EP 64
- **Instagram caption:** Same bits, totally different number. Follow @YOUR_IG_HANDLE for a new C lesson daily. Full episode: link in bio. #cprogramming #debugging #kodingkonkani #learntocode #codinglife

- **Length / source:** 0:30 · built from scenes 4 and 10

- **Script:** Quiz. A struct with char, int, char is 12 bytes. Can you make it smaller without removing anything? Three, two, one. Put the int first! int, char, char is 8 bytes. Biggest members first, less padding. Now your challenge: compare a struct and a union with the same members. Predict the sizes.
